This is consistent with our theoretical results, that is,
X_{\lambda}(t)\asymp t^{\frac{1}{\beta(\alpha+1)}}
increases as
\beta
decreases. Additionally, the width of the solution grows as time increases when acceleration occurs: it is the flattening effect
[
24
,
7
,
9
]
.
When
\beta
is large enough, as shown in Figure
6
(c) and Figure
7
(c), the solution propagates at a finite rate.

(a)
\beta=0.35

(b)
\beta=0.45

(c)
\beta=2

) with the dispersal kernel

at different times for

and different values of

. The threshold for acceleration is
\beta=\frac{1}{\alpha+1}=\frac{5}{6}
.

and different values of

. The threshold for acceleration is

.

5.3.2
Algebraic kernel

To illustrate theoretical results in Theorem
1.7
for algebraic kernel, we take
J(|z|)=\frac{1}{|z|^{1+2s}}
with
s>0
for
|z|\geq 1
. One may observe that for
\left[\frac{N}{L}\right]+1\leq n\leq N
,

{\Delta}^{2}_{n}=\int_{y_{n-1}}^{y_{n}}y^{1-2s}dy=\begin{cases}\frac{({\Delta}x)^{2-2s}}{2-2s}\left(n^{2-2s}-(n-1)^{2-2s}\right),&\text{as }s\neq 1,\\
\ln\frac{n}{n-1},&\text{as }s=1,\end{cases}

while, for
1\leq n\leq\left[\frac{N}{L}\right]
,

{\Delta}^{2}_{n}=y_{n}-y_{n-1}={\Delta}x.

The constant
\mathcal{J}_{L}
can be written explicitly as

\mathcal{J}_{L}=\int_{L}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{y^{1+2s}}dy=\frac{L^{2s}}{2s}.

For this type of dispersal kernel
J
, Figures
6
and
7
show that acceleration always occurs, and that decreasing
s
leads to an increase in the propagation speed. These observations are consistent with our theoretical findings, which show that
\ln X_{\lambda}(t)\sim_{t\to\infty}\frac{[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}{2s}
increases as
s
decreases. We can also observe the flattening effect, which becomes more pronounced as
s
decreases. Therefore, the tail of the dispersal kernel
J
plays a key role in the propagation of the solution to (
1.1
), and as the tail becomes heavier, the propagation accelerates.

(a)
s=0.5

(b)
s=1

(c)
s=2

1.1
) with the dispersal kernel
J(z)=\frac{1}{z^{1+2s}}
at different times for
\alpha=0.5
and different values of
s
.

(a)
s=0.5

(b)
s=1

Numerical approximations of the solution to (

) with the dispersal kernel
J(z)=\frac{1}{z^{1+2s}}
at different times for
\alpha=1
and different values of
s
.

Appendix A
Technical proofs: case of an algebraic acceleration

In this appendix, we collect all the technical proofs related to the construction of super- and sub-solution we are using in
Section

3
.

A.1
The upper bound

Let us start with the proof of Lemma
3.1
,

Proof of Lemma
3.1
.

A direct calculation shows that for
x>L
,

v_{0}^{\prime}(x)=C_{L}(-\beta+px^{-\beta})x^{p+\beta-1}e^{-x^{\beta}}\asymp-x^{p+\beta-1}e^{-x^{\beta}}.

(A.1)

When
y=v_{0}(x)
, we have for all
x\geq L
,

1\leq\frac{x^{\beta}}{\ln\frac{C_{L}}{y}}=\frac{x^{\beta}}{x^{\beta}-p\ln x}\to 1\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as }x\to+\infty,

and

\frac{p}{\beta}\leq\frac{x^{\beta}-\ln\frac{C_{L}}{y}}{\ln\ln\frac{e^{L^{\beta}}}{y}}=\frac{p\ln x}{\beta\ln x+\ln\left(1-\frac{p\ln x-p\ln L}{x^{\beta}}\right)}\to\frac{p}{\beta}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as }x\to+\infty.

As a result, we achieve for any
y\in(0,1)
,

\left[\ln\frac{C_{L}}{y}+\frac{p}{\beta}\ln\ln\frac{e^{L^{\beta}}}{y}\right]^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\leq v_{0}^{-1}(y)\leq\left[\ln\frac{C_{L}}{y}+\left(\frac{p}{\beta}+\gamma\right)\ln\ln\frac{e^{L^{\beta}}}{y}\right],

for some positive constant
\gamma>0
.
Therefore, by the above estimate and (
A.1
), for all
y\in(0,1)
, we get

(v_{0}^{\prime}\circ v_{0}^{-1})(y)\gtrsim-\lvert\ln y\rvert^{\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}}y.

This proves part
(i)
in Lemma
3.1
.

Since for all
t\in[t^{\star},\infty)
and
\Lambda\in(0,2]
,

0<\exp\left\{1-[\rho(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}<1,

(A.2)

then by the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
and (
3.3
), we have

x_{\Lambda}(t)\asymp_{\Lambda}t^{\frac{1}{\beta(1+\alpha)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }t\in[t^{\star},\infty)\text{ and }\Lambda\in(0,e].

By the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
and the chain rule, we get

\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}=\frac{\frac{(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha}}{\Lambda}[\rho(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}]^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}\exp\Big[1-\Big(\rho(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\Big]}{(v_{0}^{\prime}\circ v_{0}^{-1})\Big\{\exp\Big[1-\Big(\rho(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\Big]\Big\}}.

It follows from (
3.4
) that

\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}\lesssim-t^{\frac{1}{\beta(\alpha+1)}-1}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }t\in[t^{\star},\infty)\text{ and }\Lambda\in(0,2].

Therefore, by the mean value theorem, for any
0<\Lambda_{1}<\Lambda_{2}\leq 2
, there exists
\xi\in(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
such that, for all
t\in[t^{\star},\infty)
,

x_{\Lambda_{1}}(t)-x_{\Lambda_{2}}(t)=-(\Lambda_{2}-\Lambda_{1})\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}\vline_{\Lambda=\xi}\gtrsim_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}}t^{\frac{1}{\beta(\alpha+1)}-1}.

This proves part
(ii)
in Lemma
3.1
and ends the proof.

\Box

Next, we prove Lemma
3.2

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

By the definitions of
w
and
x_{\Lambda}
, for any
\Lambda\in(0,2]
and
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
, we have

(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}-\rho(\alpha+1)t\geq(1-\ln v_{0}(x_{\Lambda}(t)))^{\alpha+1}-\rho(\alpha+1)t=(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}\geq 0,

and it follows that, for any
\Lambda\in(0,2]
, the function
w
is well-defined on
[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
.

By (
A.2
) and the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
, it is easy to get that
x_{\Lambda}(t)\geq L
for any
t\in[t^{\star},\infty)
.
Let us denote
\varphi_{0}=-\ln v_{0}
. By some direct calculations, for all
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
, we get

\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}=-\frac{w}{(1-\ln w)^{\alpha}}\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(1+\varphi_{0})^{\alpha},

(A.3)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}w}{\partial x^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{w}{(1-\ln w)^{\alpha}}\Big\{(\varphi_{0}^{\prime})^{2}(1+\varphi_{0})^{2\alpha}\left[(1-\ln w)^{-\alpha}+\alpha(1-\ln w)^{-(\alpha+1)}-\alpha(1+\varphi_{0})^{-(\alpha+1)}\right]

(A.4)

\displaystyle-\varphi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(1+\varphi_{0})^{\alpha}\Big\}.

Since for
x>L
,
\varphi_{0}^{\prime}=(\beta-px^{-\beta})x^{\beta-1}\geq 0
, we get that,
for any
t\in[t^{\star},\infty)
,
w(t,\cdot)
is non-increasing on
[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
, and it follows that

w(t,x)\leq w(t,x_{\Lambda}(t))=\Lambda\leq 2\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty).

Since
\left[(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}-\rho(\alpha+1)t\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\leq 1-\ln v_{0}(x)
, it follows from the definition of
w
that

w(t,x)\geq v_{0}(x)\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty).

Thus, we get

0<(1+\varphi_{0})^{-(\alpha+1)}\leq(1-\ln w)^{-(\alpha+1)}\leq\ln^{-(\alpha+1)}\left(\frac{e}{2}\right).

(A.5)

It follows from
0<w\leq 2
for all
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
that, for any
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
, one has

0<(1-\ln w)^{-\alpha}+\alpha(1-\ln w)^{-(\alpha+1)}-\alpha(1+\varphi_{0})^{-(\alpha+1)}<2\ln^{-(\alpha+1)}\left(\frac{e}{2}\right).

Since for
x\geq L
,
\varphi(x)=-\ln v_{0}(x)=x^{\beta}-p\ln x-\ln C_{L}
and
0<\beta<\frac{1}{\alpha+1}
, we get

0\leq(\varphi_{0}^{\prime})^{2}(1+\varphi_{0})^{2\alpha}=\left(\beta x^{-(1-\beta)}-px^{-1}\right)^{2}\left(1-\ln C_{L}+x^{\beta}-p\ln x\right)^{2\alpha}\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{0}}{4\ln^{-(\alpha+1)}\left(\frac{e}{2}\right)},

and

0\leq-\varphi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(1+\varphi_{0})=\left(\beta(1-\beta)x^{-(2-\beta)}-px^{-2}\right)\left(1-\ln C_{L}+x^{\beta}-p\ln x\right)^{\alpha}\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{0}}{2},

(A.6)

for some positive constant
\mathcal{C}_{0}
.
Therefore, we conclude that, for any
t\in[t^{\star},\infty)
,
w(t,\cdot)
is convex on
[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
, and there is some positive constant
\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that for
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
,

0\leq\frac{\partial^{2}w}{\partial x^{2}}(t,x)\leq\mathcal{C}_{0}\frac{w(t,x)}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha}}.

To complete this lemma, for
t\geq t^{\star}
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
, we shall prove that
\frac{\partial^{2}\ln w(t,x)}{\partial x^{2}}\geq 0
, that is,

\frac{1}{w^{2}}\left[w\frac{\partial^{2}w}{\partial x^{2}}-\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}\right)^{2}\right]\geq 0.

It follows from (
A.3
) and (
A.4
) that

w\frac{\partial^{2}w}{\partial x^{2}}-\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}\right)^{2}=\frac{w^{2}}{(1-\ln w)^{\alpha}}\Bigg\{\alpha(\varphi_{0}^{\prime})^{2}(1+\varphi_{0})^{2\alpha}\left[(1-\ln w)^{-(\alpha+1)}-(1+\varphi_{0})^{-(\alpha+1)}\right]-\varphi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(1+\varphi_{0})^{\alpha}\Bigg\}.

Therefore, by (
A.5
) and (
A.6
), we achieve
\frac{\partial^{2}\ln w}{\partial x^{2}}\geq 0
for
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
.

\Box

Last, we provide a proof for Lemma
3.3
:

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

Unless otherwise specified, throughout the proof of Lemma
3.3
, we assume that
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{1}(t)+1,+\infty)
.
By the definition of
w
, for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
, we write

\displaystyle\frac{1-\ln w(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln w(t,x)}=\left\{1-\frac{(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[1-\left(1-\frac{x^{\beta}-x^{\beta}(1-z)^{\beta}+p\ln(1-z)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right]\right\}^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}.

Since
z\mapsto x^{\beta}-x^{\beta}(1-z)^{\beta}+p\ln(1-z)
is increasing on
\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
, we obtain, for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
,

0\leq\frac{x^{\beta}-x^{\beta}(1-z)^{\beta}+p\ln(1-z)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\leq\frac{x^{\beta}-p\ln x-x_{1}^{\beta}(t)+p\ln x_{1}(t)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}=1-\frac{1-\ln v_{0}(x_{1}(t))}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\leq 1,

(A.7)

it follows that

\displaystyle 0<\frac{(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[1-\left(1-\frac{x^{\beta}-x^{\beta}(1-z)^{\beta}+p\ln(1-z)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}-\rho(\alpha+1)t]-[(1-\ln v_{0}(x_{1}(t)))^{\alpha+1}-\rho(\alpha+1)t]}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\leq 1.

Since
\beta\in(0,1)
and
\frac{1}{\alpha+1}\in(0,1)
, by using the inequality
(1-y)^{n}\geq 1-ny-(1-n)y^{2}
for all
y\in[0,1]
and some
n\in(0,1)
7
7

7

Let us give a quick proof. For
n\in(0,1)
, denote
h(y):=(1-y)^{n}-1+ny+(1-n)y^{2}
on
[0,1]
. By a direct calculation,
h^{\prime}(y)=-n(1-y)^{n-1}+n+2(1-n)y
and
h^{\prime\prime}(y)=-n(1-n)(1-y)^{-(2-n)}+2(1-n)
. Observe that, for
h^{\prime\prime}
, there is a unique root
y_{1}:=1-\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2-n}}\in(0,1)
, and that
h^{\prime\prime}>0
on
\left(0,y_{1}\right)
and
h^{\prime\prime}<0
on
\left(y_{1},1\right)
, that is,
h^{\prime}
is strictly increasing on
\left(0,y_{1}\right)
and strictly decreasing on
\left(y_{1},1\right)
. It follows from the facts that
h^{\prime}(0)=0
,
h^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)=-n2^{1-n}+2>0
and
h^{\prime}(y)\to-\infty
as
y\to 1^{-}
that there is a unique root
y_{2}\in(0,1)
such that
h^{\prime}(y_{2})=0
, and thus,
h^{\prime}>0
on
(0,y_{2})
and
h^{\prime}<0
on
(y_{2},1)
, that is,
h
is strictly increasing on
\left(0,y_{2}\right)
and strictly decreasing on
\left(y_{2},1\right)
. Therefore, we have
h(y)\geq\min\{h(0),h(1)\}=0
, that is,
(1-y)^{n}-1+ny+(1-n)y^{2}\geq 0
on
[0,1]
.
Moreover, since
ny+(1-n)y^{2}\leq y
for all
n\in(0,1)
and
y\in[0,1]
, we have
(1-y)^{n}\geq 1-y
for all
y\in[0,1]
.
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1-\ln w(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln w(t,x)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}\frac{(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[1-\left(1-\frac{x^{\beta}-x^{\beta}(1-z)^{\beta}+p\ln(1-z)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}\left\{\frac{(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[1-\left(1-\frac{x^{\beta}-x^{\beta}(1-z)^{\beta}+p\ln(1-z)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right]\right\}^{2}.

In view of the definitions of
x_{\Lambda}
and
C_{L}
, one may notice that
x_{1}(t)\geq L\geq\left(\frac{e}{C_{L}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
, and thus,

1-\ln v_{0}(x)\leq x^{\beta}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{1}(t)+1,+\infty).

(A.8)

By using (
A.7
) and the inequality
(1-y)^{\alpha+1}\geq 1-(\alpha+1)y
for
y\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq 1-\left(1-\frac{x^{\beta}-x^{\beta}(1-z)^{\beta}+p\ln(1-z)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\right)^{\alpha+1}

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha+1)\frac{x^{\beta}\left(1-(1-z)^{\beta}\right)+p\ln(1-z)}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha+1)\frac{x^{\beta}}{1-\ln v_{0}(x)}\left(1-(1-z)^{\beta}\right),

and thus, by (
A.8
) and using the inequalities
(1-y)^{n}\geq 1-ny-(1-n)y^{2}
and
(1-y)^{n}\geq 1-y
for all
y\in[0,1]
and some
n\in(0,1)
(see footnote
7
), we achieve, for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1-\ln w(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln w(t,x)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{x^{\alpha\beta+\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left(1-(1-z)^{\beta}\right)-\frac{\alpha(\alpha+1)x^{2\alpha\beta+2\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{2\alpha+2}}\left(1-(1-z)^{\beta}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\beta x^{\alpha\beta+\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}z-C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{x^{2\alpha\beta+2\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{2\alpha+2}}z^{2},

where
C_{\alpha,\beta}:=1-\beta+\alpha(\alpha+1)>0
.
Therefore, for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{w(t,x(1-z))}{w(t,x)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{(1-\ln w(t,x))\left(1-\frac{1-\ln w(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln w(t,x)}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\frac{x^{\alpha\beta+\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha}}\left(1-(1-z)^{\beta}\right)+\alpha(\alpha+1)\frac{x^{2\alpha\beta+2\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{2\alpha+1}}\left(1-(1-z)^{\beta}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\frac{\beta x^{\alpha\beta+\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{\alpha}}z+C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{x^{2\alpha\beta+2\beta}}{(1-\ln w(t,x))^{2\alpha+1}}z^{2}\right\}.

This completes the proof.

\Box

A.2
The lower bound

In this subsection, we proof a technical Lemma, Lemma
3.7
.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

#
Let us prove (i) and
(ii)
.

By the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
, for any
\Lambda\in\left(0,e^{-1}\right)
,

x_{\Lambda}(t)\geq\ln^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\frac{1}{\Lambda}>1\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }t\geq 0,

and for any
\Lambda\in(0,1)
,

x_{\Lambda}(t)\geq Y(t):=\left\{-1+[\rho(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }t\geq\frac{1}{\rho(\alpha+1)}.

(A.9)

By the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
, one has

\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}\asymp_{\Lambda}-t^{\frac{1}{\beta(\alpha+1)}-1},

and thus, by the mean value theorem, for any
0<\Lambda_{1}<\Lambda_{2}<1
, there is
\xi\in(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
such that

x_{\Lambda_{1}}(t)-x_{\Lambda_{2}}(t)=-(\Lambda_{2}-\Lambda_{1})\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}\vline_{\Lambda=\xi}\asymp t^{\frac{1}{\beta(\alpha+1)}-1}\to+\infty\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as }t\to+\infty,

since
\alpha\beta+\beta-1<0
.

#
Let us prove (iii).
In view of the definitions of
w
and
x_{\Lambda}
, it is easy to get that, for any
\Lambda\in(0,1)
,
w
is well-defined on
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
.
Let us denote
\varphi_{0}=-\ln v_{0}
, by some direct calculations, for all
t>0
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
, we get

\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}=-\frac{w}{(1-\ln w)^{\alpha}}\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(1+\varphi_{0})^{\alpha},

(A.10)

and

\frac{\partial^{2}w}{\partial x^{2}}=\frac{w}{(1-\ln w)^{\alpha}}\Big\{(\varphi_{0}^{\prime})^{2}(1+\varphi_{0})^{2\alpha}\Big((1-\ln w)^{-\alpha}+\alpha(1-\ln w)^{-(\alpha+1)}-\alpha(1+\varphi_{0})^{-(\alpha+1)}\Big)-\varphi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(1+\varphi_{0})^{\alpha}\Big\}.

(A.11)

We then observe that for all
t\geq 0
,
w(t,\cdot)
is non-increasing on
[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
, and that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}(t,x_{\Lambda}(t))\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda}{(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha}}x_{\Lambda}^{\alpha\beta+\beta-1}(t)\left(1+\frac{1}{x_{\Lambda}^{\beta}(t)}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq Y^{\alpha\beta+\beta-1}(t)\left(1+\frac{1}{Y^{\beta}(t)}\right)^{\alpha}\to 0\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as }t\to+\infty,

by (
A.9
) and
\alpha\beta+\beta-1<0
.
Similarly to Lemma
3.2
, one may see that

\frac{\partial^{2}w}{\partial x^{2}}(t,x)\geq 0\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for all }t>0\text{ and }x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t).

#
Let us prove (iv).

By the definition of
w
, for all
t>0
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
, one may find

w(t,x)=v_{0}(x)\exp\left\{1-\ln v_{0}(x)-[(1-\ln v_{0}(x))^{\alpha+1}-\rho(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}\leq e^{-x^{\beta}+[\rho(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}.

\Box

Appendix B
Technical proofs: case of an exponential acceleration

In this appendix, we collect all the technical proofs related to the construction of super- and sub-solution we are using in
Section

4
.

B.1
The upper bound

Let us start with the proof of Lemma
4.1
,

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

The proof is very similar to Lemma
3.1
, so we streamline the arguments.

#
Proof of Part
I.

By a direct calculation, one gets for
x>L
,

v_{0}^{\prime}(x)=C_{L}(-2s+q\ln^{-1}x)\frac{\ln^{q}x}{x^{2s+1}}\asymp-\frac{\ln^{q}x}{x^{2s+1}}.

(B.1)

When
y=v_{0}(x)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{v_{0}^{-1}(y)}{C_{L}^{\frac{1}{2s}}y^{-\frac{1}{2s}}\left(\ln L-\frac{1}{2s}\ln y\right)^{\frac{q}{2s}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{x}{C_{L}^{\frac{1}{2s}}(C_{L}x^{-2s}\ln^{q}x)^{-\frac{1}{2s}}\left(\ln L-\frac{1}{2s}\ln(C_{L}x^{-2s}\ln^{q}x)\right)^{\frac{q}{2s}}}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{q}{2s}\frac{\ln\ln x-\ln\ln L}{\ln x}\right)^{-\frac{q}{2s}}\to 1\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as }x\to+\infty,

and notice that
\left(1-\frac{q}{2s}\frac{\ln\ln x-\ln\ln L}{\ln x}\right)^{-\frac{q}{2s}}\geq 1
for all
x\geq L
. This implies (
4.2
).

Collecting (
4.2
) and (
B.1
), since
x\mapsto-\frac{\ln^{q}x}{x^{2s+1}}
is increasing for
x>L
, one may obtain for
y\in(0,1)
,

(v^{\prime}\circ v^{-1})(y)\asymp-y^{1+\frac{1}{2s}}(\ln y^{-1})^{-\frac{q}{2s}},

which gives (
4.3
).

#
Proof of Part
II
.

Since for all
t\in[t^{\star},+\infty)
and
\Lambda\in(0,2]
,

0<(1+\kappa t^{p})^{-1}\exp\left\{1-\left[r(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}<1,

(B.2)

then by (
4.2
) and the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
, for any
t\geq t^{\star}
, one may get

x_{\Lambda}(t)\asymp_{\Lambda}t^{\frac{1}{2s}\left(p+\frac{q}{\alpha+1}\right)}\exp\left\{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}.

In view of (
4.3
) and the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
, for all
t\geq t^{\star}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{\Lambda}(1+\kappa t^{p})^{-1}\left[r(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}\right]^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}\exp\left\{1-\left[r(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}}{v_{0}^{\prime}\circ v_{0}^{-1}\left\{(1+\kappa t^{p})^{-1}\exp\left\{1-\left[r(\alpha+1)t+(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha+1}\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}\right\}}

\displaystyle\asymp_{\Lambda}-t^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}+\frac{1}{2s}\left(p+\frac{q}{\alpha+1}\right)}e^{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}.

Therefore, by the mean value theorem, for any
0<\Lambda_{1}<\Lambda_{2}\leq 2
, there is
\xi\in(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
such that, for all
t\geq t^{\star}
,

x_{\Lambda_{1}}(t)-x_{\Lambda_{2}}(t)=-(\Lambda_{2}-\Lambda_{1})\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}\vline_{\Lambda=\xi}\asymp_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}}t^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}+\frac{1}{2s}\left(\frac{q}{\alpha+1}+p\right)}e^{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}.

This completes the proof.

\Box

.

It follows from

Since
\frac{v_{0}^{\prime}}{v_{0}}=\left(-2s+\frac{q}{\ln x}\right)\frac{1}{x}

x\geq x_{1}(t)
,

0\leq\left(\frac{v_{0}^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{2}\{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}]\}^{2\alpha}\leq\left(-2s+\frac{q}{\ln x}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{x^{2}}\left(1-\ln\left(C_{L}\frac{\ln^{q}x}{x^{2s}}\right)\right)^{2\alpha}\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{0}}{4\ln^{-(\alpha+1)}\left(\frac{e}{2}\right)}\frac{1}{x}

and

0\leq\left(\frac{v_{0}^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\left\{1-\ln[\left(1+\kappa t^{p}\right)v_{0}]\right\}^{\alpha}\leq\left(2s-\frac{q}{\ln x}-\frac{q}{\ln^{2}x}\right)\frac{1}{x^{2}}\left(1-\ln\left(C_{L}\frac{\ln^{q}x}{x^{2s}}\right)\right)^{\alpha}\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{0}}{2}\frac{1}{x}

Therefore, by (
B.4
), we conclude that there is some constant
\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that for
t\geq t^{\star}
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
,

0\leq\frac{\partial^{2}\psi}{\partial x^{2}}(t,x)\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{0}}{x}\frac{\psi(t,x)}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha}}.

Now, let us show that, for all
t\geq t^{\star}
,
\psi(t,\cdot)
is log-convex on
[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
. By a direct calculation, one may have

\frac{\partial^{2}\ln\psi}{\partial x^{2}}=\frac{1}{\psi^{2}}\left[\psi\frac{\partial^{2}\psi}{\partial x^{2}}-\left(\frac{\partial\psi}{\partial x}\right)^{2}\right].

It follows from (
B.3
), (
B.4
) and (
B.6
) that for all
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
,

\displaystyle\psi\frac{\partial^{2}\psi}{\partial x^{2}}-\left(\frac{\partial\psi}{\partial x}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi^{2}}{(1-\ln\psi)^{\alpha}}\Bigg\{\left(\frac{v_{0}^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{2}\{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}]\}^{2\alpha}\Big\{\alpha(1-\ln\psi)^{-(\alpha+1)}

\displaystyle-\alpha[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}]]^{-(\alpha+1)}\Big\}+[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}]]^{\alpha}\left(\frac{v_{0}^{\prime}}{v_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\Bigg\}\geq 0.

Therefore, for all
t\geq t^{\star}
, we achieve
\frac{\partial^{2}\ln\psi}{\partial x^{2}}(t,x)\geq 0
for all
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
, that is,
\psi(t,\cdot)
is log-convex on
[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
.

\Box

Last, we proof our last technical Lemma, Lemma
4.3
.

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

Unless otherwise specified, throughout the proof of Lemma
4.3
, we assume that
(t,x)\in[t^{\star},\infty)\times[x_{1}(t)+1,+\infty)
.
By the definition of
\psi
, for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1-\ln\psi(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln\psi(t,x)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[1-\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left(1-\left(1-\frac{-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)}{1-\ln(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}.

Since
z\mapsto 2s\ln(1-z)-q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)
is non-increasing on
\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
, we have, for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
,

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)}{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{-2s\ln\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}}{\ln x}\right)}{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]}

(B.7)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x_{1}(t))]}{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]}\leq 1,

and thus, by the definition of
x_{1}
and
\psi
, we get

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left(1-\left(1+\frac{2s\ln(1-z)-q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)}{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left[[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha+1}-r(\alpha+1)t\right]-\left[[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x_{1}(t))]]^{\alpha+1}-r(\alpha+1)t\right]}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\leq 1.

It follows from the inequality
(1-y)^{n}\geq 1-ny-(1-n)y^{2}
for all
y\in[0,1]
and some
n\in(0,1)
(see footnote
7
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1-\ln\psi(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln\psi(t,x)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left(1-\left(1-\frac{-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)}{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}\left\{\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left(1-\left(1-\frac{-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)}{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right)\right\}^{2}.

By (
B.7
) and the inequality
(1-y)^{\alpha+1}\geq 1-(\alpha+1)y
for all
y\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha+1}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left(1-\left(1-\frac{-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)}{1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]}\right)^{\alpha+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha+1)\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha+1)\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)\right].

In view of the definition of
v_{0}
, there is a time
t^{\dagger}
such that for all
(t,x)\in[t^{\dagger},\infty)\times[x_{1}(t)+1,+\infty)
, we have

1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]\leq 2s\ln x.

Thus,
for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
and
(t,x)\in[t^{\dagger},\infty)\times[x_{1}(t)+1,+\infty)
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1-\ln\psi(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln\psi(t,x)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\alpha(\alpha+1)\left\{\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)+q\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(1-z)}{\ln x}\right)\right]\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha+1}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)\right]-\alpha(\alpha+1)\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{2\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{2\alpha+2}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1+(2s)^{\alpha+1}(\ln^{\alpha}x)\ln(1-z)-C_{\alpha}(\ln^{2\alpha}x)\ln^{2}(1-z),

where
C_{\alpha}=\alpha(\alpha+1)(2s)^{2\alpha+2}
.
Therefore, for all
z\in\left(\frac{1}{x},1-\frac{x_{1}(t)}{x}\right)
and
(t,x)\in[t^{\dagger},\infty)\times[x_{1}(t)+1,+\infty)
, we achieve

\displaystyle\frac{\psi(t,x(1-z))}{\psi(t,x)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))\left(1-\frac{1-\ln\psi(t,x(1-z))}{1-\ln\psi(t,x)}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{\alpha}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)\right]+\alpha(\alpha+1)\frac{[1-\ln[(1+\kappa t^{p})v_{0}(x)]]^{2\alpha}}{(1-\ln\psi(t,x))^{2\alpha+1}}\left[-2s\ln(1-z)\right]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{-(2s)^{\alpha+1}(\ln^{\alpha}x)\ln(1-z)+C_{\alpha}(\ln^{2\alpha}x)\ln^{2}(1-z)\right\}.

This completes the proof.

\Box

B.2
The lower bound

Firstly, we prove Lemma
4.6
.

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

Denote
Y_{0}(t):=e^{-\frac{1}{s}}e^{\frac{1}{2s}\left[r(\alpha+1)t\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}
for all
t>0
and
t_{0}:=\frac{2^{\alpha+1}}{r(\alpha+1)}
. Let us define

\varphi_{log}(t,x):=(1+2s\ln x)^{\alpha+1}-r(\alpha+1)[t-h(t,x)],

for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty)\times[Y_{0}(t),+\infty)
, satisfying
\varphi=\exp\{1-\varphi_{log}^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\}
.
By some direct calculations, since
\kappa\leq\frac{[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{2^{\alpha+1}rpe^{2p}}
, for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty)\times[Y_{0}(t),+\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\varphi_{log}}{\partial x}

\displaystyle=2s(\alpha+1)\frac{1}{x}\left[(1+2s\ln x)^{\alpha}-rp\kappa\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{p[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x^{2sp}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 2s(\alpha+1)\left\{\left[[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}-t^{-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right]^{\alpha}-rpe^{2p}\kappa\right\}\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{x}

\displaystyle\geq 2s(\alpha+1)\left\{2^{-\alpha}[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}-rpe^{2p}\kappa\right\}\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{x}>0.

As a result, for all fixed
t\geq t_{0}
,
\varphi_{log}(t,\cdot)
is strictly increasing on
[Y_{0}(t),+\infty]
. One may notice that for any
t\geq t_{0}
, since
\kappa\leq\frac{1}{2e^{2p}[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{log}(t,Y_{0}(t))

\displaystyle=[r(\alpha+1)t]\left\{\left[1-[r(\alpha+1)t]^{-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right]^{\alpha+1}-1+e^{2p}\kappa t^{-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq[r(\alpha+1)]\left\{-[r(\alpha+1)]^{-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}+e^{2p}\kappa\right\}t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}<0,

and, denoting
Y_{1}(t):=e^{-\frac{1}{2s}+\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}
,

\varphi_{log}(t,Y_{1}(t))=r(\alpha+1)h(t,Y_{1}(t))>0.

Thus, for all
t\geq t_{0}
, there exists a unique
Y(t)\in(Y_{0}(t),Y_{1}(t))
such that
\varphi_{log}(t,Y(t))=0
and
\varphi_{log}(t,x)>0
for all
x\in(Y(t),+\infty)
. It therefore follows from the definition of
\varphi
that, for all
t\geq t_{0}
,
\varphi(t,x)
is well-defined if only if
x\in[Y(t),+\infty)
and
\varphi(t,\cdot)
is strictly decreasing on
[Y(t),+\infty)
. Thus, by the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
, one may have
\varphi_{log}(t,x_{e}(t))=0
, which implies
x_{e}(t)=Y(t)
. Therefore, it follows from the fact that, for all
t\geq t_{0}
,
\varphi(t,\cdot)
is strictly decreasing on
[x_{e}(t),+\infty)
that for any
\Lambda\in(0,e)
and all
t\geq t_{0}
, the position
x_{\Lambda}(t)
is decreasing in
\Lambda
unique such that
\varphi(t,x_{\Lambda}(t))=\Lambda
and

x_{\Lambda}(t)\geq x_{e}(t)>Y_{0}(t)=e^{-\frac{1}{s}}e^{\frac{1}{2s}\left[r(\alpha+1)t\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}},

which gives (
4.20
).

Let us show that
\varphi(t,x)
is strictly increasing in
t
for all
t
large enough and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
. By the definition of
h
, for
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
, by (
4.20
), there is
\tilde{t}_{1}\geq t_{0}
such that for all
t\geq\tilde{t}_{1}
, we have

\frac{\partial h}{\partial t}=\kappa\frac{e^{p[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x^{2sp}}\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}t^{-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right)\leq\kappa\frac{e^{p[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x_{\Lambda}^{2sp}(t)}\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}t^{-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right)\leq 2e^{2p}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\kappa,

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}:=\frac{p}{\alpha+1}[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}
.
As a result, since
\kappa\leq\frac{1}{4\tilde{\gamma}_{1}e^{2p}}
, for all
t\geq\tilde{t}_{1}
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
, we get

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial t}=\frac{r\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\left[1-\frac{\partial h}{\partial t}\right]\geq r\left(1-2e^{2p}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\kappa\right)\frac{\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}>0.

Let us now show that, for
t
large enough,
\varphi(t,\cdot)
is convex on
[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
. By the definition of
h
, we have

\frac{\partial h}{\partial x}=-2sp\kappa\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{p[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x^{2sp+1}},

and thus, for any
(t,x)\in[t_{0},\infty)\times[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
, by (
4.20
), we have

\frac{\partial^{2}h}{\partial x^{2}}=2sp(2sp+1)\kappa\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{p[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x^{2sp+2}}\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\kappa\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{x^{2}},

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{2}:=2sp(2sp+1)e^{2p}
.
Some direct calculations show that

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x}=\frac{\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\left[-2s\frac{(1+2s\ln x)^{\alpha}}{x}-r\frac{\partial h}{\partial x}\right],

(B.8)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi}{\partial x^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\Bigg\{\left[2s\frac{(1+2s\ln x)^{\alpha}}{x}+r\frac{\partial h}{\partial x}\right]^{2}\left[(1-\ln\varphi)^{-\alpha}+\alpha(1-\ln\varphi)^{-1}\right]

(B.9)

\displaystyle+\left[2s\frac{(1+2s\ln x)^{\alpha}}{x^{2}}\left(1-2s\alpha(1+2s\ln x)^{-1}\right)-r\frac{\partial^{2}h}{\partial x^{2}}\right]\Bigg\}.

By substituting
\frac{\partial h}{\partial x}
and
\frac{\partial^{2}h}{\partial x^{2}}
into (
B.9
), for any
\Lambda\in(0,e)
there is
\tilde{t}_{2}\geq t_{0}
such that for all
t\geq\tilde{t}_{2}
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi}{\partial x^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\Bigg\{2s\frac{(1+2s\ln x)^{\alpha}}{x^{2}}\left[1-2s\alpha(1+2s\ln x)^{-1}\right]-r\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\kappa\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{x^{2}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{x^{2}}\frac{\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\Bigg\{2s\frac{\left[-1+[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right]^{\alpha}}{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}\left[1-2s\alpha\left[-1+[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right]^{-1}\right]-r\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\kappa\Bigg\}\geq 0,

since
\kappa\leq\frac{s[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{2^{\alpha}r\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}
.

Therefore, by taking
\tilde{t}:=\max\{t_{0},\tilde{t}_{1},\tilde{t}_{2}\}
, for any
\Lambda\in(0,e)
, we conclude that for any
t\geq\tilde{t}
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
,
\varphi(t,x)
is well-defined, strictly increasing in
t
, and strictly decreasing and convex in
x
.

\Box

Next, we prove Lemma
4.7
.

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

By the inequality
a^{q}+b^{q}\geq(a+b)^{q}
for any
a>0
,
b>0
and
q\in(0,1)
, one may obtain

\left[\left(1+2s\ln x\right)^{\alpha+1}-r(\alpha+1)t\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\geq 1+2s\ln x-[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}.

Thus, by the definition of
\varphi
, one has

\varphi(t,x)\leq\frac{e^{[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x^{2s}}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ for all }t>\tilde{t}\text{ and }x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t).

On the other hand, one may notice that
t-h(t,x)\geq 0
for all
t\geq\tilde{t}
and
x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t)
, and it follows from the definition of
\varphi
that

\varphi(t,x)\geq\frac{1}{x^{2s}}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ for all }t>\tilde{t}\text{ and }x\geq x_{\Lambda}(t).

In view of the definition of
\varphi
, some direct calculations show that

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial t}=\frac{r\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\left[1-\frac{\partial h}{\partial t}\right]\leq\frac{r\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\left[1-\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\kappa\frac{e^{p[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x^{2sp}}\right],

where
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}=\frac{p}{\alpha+1}[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}
.
Thus, by (
4.21
), one may notice that

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial t}\leq\frac{r\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}(1-\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\kappa\varphi^{p}),

and by (
B.8
), since
y\mapsto y(1-2s\ln y)^{\alpha}
is non-decreasing for
0<y\leq e^{\frac{1}{2s}-\alpha}
, one may have for
\varphi<\min\{e^{\frac{1}{2s}-\alpha},e\}
,

\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x}\geq-2s\frac{\varphi}{(1-\ln\varphi)^{\alpha}}\frac{(1+2s\ln x)^{\alpha}}{x}\geq-2s\varphi^{1+\frac{1}{2s}}.

By (
4.20
), (
B.8
) and the definition of
x_{\Lambda}(t)
, for any
\Lambda\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle 0\geq\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x}(t,x_{\Lambda}(t))

\displaystyle\geq-2s\frac{\Lambda}{(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha}}\frac{\left[1+2s\ln x_{\Lambda}(t)\right]^{\alpha}}{x_{\Lambda}(t)}

\displaystyle\geq-2se^{\frac{1}{s}}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{-\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}\to 0\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as }t\to\infty,

and thus,

\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x}(t,x_{\Lambda}(t))=0\hskip 9.24994pt\text{uniformly for }\Lambda\in(0,1).

\Box

Now, we prove Lemma
4.8
.

Proof of Lemma
4.8
.

The lower bound of
x_{\Lambda}(t)
has already been given by (
4.20
). Let us now show the upper bound of
x_{\Lambda}(t)
.
Since

\left(1+2s\ln x\right)^{\alpha+1}-r(\alpha+1)[t-h(t,x)]\geq\ln^{\alpha+1}\left(x^{2s}\right)-r(\alpha+1)t,

by the inequality
(a+b)^{q}\leq a^{q}+b^{q}
for some
a>0
,
b>0
and
q\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi\left(t,\bar{C}_{\Lambda}e^{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{1-\left[\ln^{\alpha+1}\left(\bar{C}_{\Lambda}^{2s}e^{[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}\right)-r(\alpha+1)t\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e}{\bar{C}_{\Lambda}^{2s}}\leq\Lambda=\varphi(t,x_{\Lambda}(t)),

as long as
\bar{C}_{\Lambda}\geq\left(\frac{e}{\Lambda}\right)^{\frac{1}{2s}}
, and then, since
\varphi(t,\cdot)
is decreasing on
[x_{\Lambda}(t),+\infty)
for all
t\geq\tilde{t}
, we obtain for any
\bar{C}_{\Lambda}\geq\left(\frac{e}{\Lambda}\right)^{\frac{1}{2s}}
,

x_{\Lambda}(t)\leq\bar{C}_{\Lambda}e^{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}},

which completes the estimate of
x_{\Lambda}
.

Let us denote

\mathcal{X}(x,y):=\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2s}+\frac{1}{2s}\left[(1-\ln x)^{\alpha+1}+r(\alpha+1)\left[t-h(t,y)\right]\right]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\}.

Some direct calculations show that

\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial x}=-\frac{(1-\ln x)^{\alpha}}{2sx}\frac{\mathcal{X}}{(1+2s\ln\mathcal{X})^{\alpha}},

and

\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial y}=-\frac{r}{2s}\frac{\partial h}{\partial y}\frac{\mathcal{X}}{(1+2s\ln\mathcal{X})^{\alpha}}.

For any
\Lambda\in(0,1)
, notice that

x_{\Lambda}(t)=\mathcal{X}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t)).

Then, by the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
, we have

\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial x}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t))+\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial y}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t)).

By (
4.20
), for large time
t
, say
t\geq\tilde{t}_{3}
, we get

\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial x}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t))=-\frac{(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha}}{2s\Lambda}\frac{x_{\Lambda}(t)}{(1+2s\ln x_{\Lambda}(t))^{\alpha}}\lesssim_{\Lambda}-t^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}},

and since
\kappa=\kappa_{0}\leq\frac{[r(\alpha+1)]^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}}{2^{\alpha+1}rpe^{2p}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial y}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t))

\displaystyle=rp\kappa\frac{t^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{p[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}}{x_{\Lambda}(t)^{2sp}}\frac{1}{(1+2s\ln x_{\Lambda}(t))^{\alpha}}\leq 2rpe^{2p}[r(\alpha+1)]^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}\kappa\leq\frac{1}{2^{\alpha}}<1.

Thus, for all
t\geq\tilde{t}_{3}
, we get

\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}=\frac{\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial x}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t))}{1-\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial y}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t))}=\frac{-\frac{(1-\ln\Lambda)^{\alpha}}{2s\Lambda}\frac{x_{\Lambda}(t)}{(1+2s\ln x_{\Lambda}(t))^{\alpha}}}{1-\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial y}(\Lambda,x_{\Lambda}(t))}\lesssim_{\Lambda}-t^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}.

Therefore, for any
0<\Lambda_{1}<\Lambda_{2}<1
, by the definition of
x_{\Lambda}
and the mean value theorem, there is
\xi\in(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
such that

x_{\Lambda_{1}}(t)-x_{\Lambda_{2}}(t)=(\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2})\frac{\partial x_{\Lambda}(t)}{\partial\Lambda}\vline_{\Lambda=\xi}\gtrsim_{\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2},\xi}t^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+1}}e^{\frac{1}{2s}[r(\alpha+1)t]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}}\to+\infty,

as
t\to\infty
, which completes the proof of the lemma.

\Box

Lastly, we prove Lemma
4.9
.

Proof of Lemma
4.9
.

Let

g_{\varepsilon}(y):=\begin{cases}y,&y\in\left[0,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right],\\
\exp\left\{1-[(1-\ln y)^{\alpha+1}+P(y)]^{\frac{1}{\alpha+1}}\right\},&y\in\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right),\\
\varepsilon,&y\in\left[\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon,1\right],\end{cases}

satisfying

g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(y)=\begin{cases}1,&y\in\left[0,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right],\\
\frac{g_{\varepsilon}}{(1-\ln g_{\varepsilon})^{\alpha}}\frac{(1-\ln y)^{\alpha}}{y}\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha+1}\frac{y}{(1-\ln y)^{\alpha}}P^{\prime}(y)\right),&y\in\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right),\\
0,&y\in\left[\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon,1\right],\end{cases}

where we here select a function
P\in C^{2}\left(\left[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right]\right)
with
P\geq 0
and
P^{\prime}\geq 0
on
\left[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right]
such that

\displaystyle P\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)=0,\hskip 9.24994ptP\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)=(1-\ln\varepsilon)^{\alpha+1}-\left(1-\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)\right)^{\alpha+1},

\displaystyle P^{\prime}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)=0\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994ptP^{\prime}\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)=(\alpha+1)\frac{\left(1-\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)\right)^{\alpha}}{\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon}.

Thus, by the choice of
P
, one may notice that

g_{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)=\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\hskip 9.24994ptg_{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)=\varepsilon,\hskip 9.24994ptg_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)=1\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994ptg_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)=0,

and then, one gets
g_{\varepsilon}\in C^{1}([0,1])
. Also, by choosing appropriate value for
P^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)
and
P^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)
such that
g^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)=0
and
g^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon\right)=0
. As a result, under the choice of
P
, one has
g_{\varepsilon}\in C^{2}([0,1])
.

One may then get that, for all
y\in(0,1)
,

g_{\varepsilon}(y)\leq y,\hskip 9.24994ptg_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(y)\frac{y}{(1-\ln y)^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{g_{\varepsilon}(y)}{(1-\ln g_{\varepsilon}(y))^{\alpha}}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt0\leq g^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(y)\leq 1.

Moreover, since
g^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\in C([0,1])
, there is some constant
C_{\varepsilon}
, depending on
\varepsilon
, such that

g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}\geq-C_{\varepsilon}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for some }C_{\varepsilon}>0.

\Box
